ax[4,0].set_ylabel('Speed (cm/s)')\n        ax[5,0].set_ylabel('Lick')\n        \n        plt.tight_layout()\n        plt.show()\n        if figfile is not None:\n            fig.savefig(figfile)\n        \n    return neural, input, output\n\ndef survey(force=False, datadir=DATADIR, surveyfile=SURVEYFILE):\n    if not force and os.path.exists(surveyfile):\n        print(f\"Loading existing survey from {surveyfile}\")\n        with open(surveyfile, 'rb') as f:\n            return pickle.load(f)\n    all_info = {}\n    subjects = get_subjects(datadir=datadir)\n    all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n\n    for i,subject in enumerate(subjects):\n        sessions = get_sessions(subject, datadir=datadir)\n        print(f\"Subject: {subject}, Sessions: {sessions}\")\n        all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.zeros((0,2))\n        for session in sessions:\n            info = survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=datadir)\n            print(f\"Subject {subject}, Session {session}:\")\n            for key, value in info.items():\n                print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n            for key,value in info.items():\n                if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n                    all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.concatenate((all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject], value), axis=0)\n                elif key == 'neural_time_bin_size':\n                    if 'neural_time_bin_size' not in all_info:\n                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'] = [value,]\n                    else:\n                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'].append(value)\n                elif not ('range' in key or 'unique' in key):\n                    if 'range_'+key not in all_info:\n                        all_info['range_'+key] = (value, value)\n                    else:\n                        all_info['range_'+key] = (min(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[0], value), max(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[1], value))\n                elif key not in all_info:\n                    all_info[key] = value\n                elif 'range' in key:\n                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0], value[0]), max(all_info[key][1], value[1]))\n                elif 'unique' in key:\n                    all_info[key] = np.unique(np.concatenate((all_info[key], value)))\n                else:\n                    raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected key in info: {key}\")\n        # plot a horizontal line from reward_zone_positions[0] to reward_zone_positions[1] for each trial, with y value equal to the trial number\n        reward_zone_positions = all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject]\n        for trial in range(reward_zone_positions.shape[0]):\n            ax[i].plot(reward_zone_positions[trial], [trial, trial], '-')\n        ax[i].set_title(f\"Subject {subject}\")\n\n                    \n    print(\"Summary across all sessions:\")\n    for key, value in all_info.items():\n        if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n            continue\n        elif 'range' in key:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value[0]} to {value[1]}\")\n        elif 'unique' in key:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n        else:\n            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n            \n    ax[0].set_xlabel('Position (cm)')\n    ax[0].set_ylabel('Trial')\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.png')\n all_info['reward_zone_labels'] = {k: v['zone_labels'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    all_info['reward_zone_states'] = {k: v['states'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    all_info['reward_zone_dist'] = {k: v['zone_dist'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n    \n    with open(surveyfile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(all_info, f)\n        \n    return all_info\n\ndef segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n    \n\ndef main():\n\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n    parser.add_argument('outpicklefile', type=str, nargs='?', choose max_sessions random sessions across all subjects\n        all_sessions = [(subject, session) for subject, sessions in sessions_per_subject.items() for session in sessions]\n        chosen_sessions = np.random.choice(len(all_sessions), size=max_sessions, replace=False)\n        sessions_per_subject = {subject: [session for i, (s, session) in enumerate(all_sessions) if s == subject and i in chosen_sessions] for subject in data['subjects']}\n\n    if show_processing:\n        if args.sample:\n            show_sessions_per_subject = sessions_per_subject\n        else:\n            # all CA1\n            trial_offset += len(neural)\n    data['subject_idx'] = np.array(data['subject_idx'])\n    with open(args.outpicklefile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(data, f)\n    print(f\"Saved converted data to {args.outpicklefile}\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n","numLines":920,"startLine":1,"totalLines":920}}}

The reward zone identity is determined by parsing the NWB file's `identifier` field to extract the scene/environment schedule, then applying a rule that the zone switches after the first 30 trials on switch days.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef scene_from_identifier(identifier: str) -> str:\n    return identifier.rstrip(\"/\").split(\"/\")[-1]\n\ndef scene_zone_labels(scene: str) -> list[str]:\n    labels: list[str] = []\n    for token in scene.split(\"_\"):\n        if token in ZONE_BOUNDS:\n            labels.append(token)\n        elif token.startswith(\"Location\") and token[-1:] in ZONE_BOUNDS:\n            labels.append(token[-1])\n    ...\n\ndef zones_by_trial(scene: str, n_trials: int) -> list[str]:\n    labels = scene_zone_labels(scene)\n    if len(labels) == 1:\n        return [labels[0]] * n_trials\n    return [labels[0]] * 30 + [labels[1]] * (n_trials - 30)\n```\n\niii. The AI parses the NWB identifier to derive zone labels (A/B/C), with the same zone coordinates from the paper (A=80-130, B=200-250, C=320-370 cm). This is a different approach from the reference (which uses a Viterbi algorithm on reward_zone positions), but derives from the same experimental schedule information.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance from position to the nearest edge of the active reward zone. Distance is negative before the zone, zero inside the zone, and positive after the zone.\n\nii.\n```python\nZONE_BOUNDS = {\"A\": (80.0, 130.0), \"B\": (200.0, 250.0), \"C\": (320.0, 370.0)}\n\ndef reward_distance(position: np.ndarray, zone: str) -> np.ndarray:\n    start, stop = ZONE_BOUNDS[zone]\n    return np.where(position < start, position - start,\n                    np.where(position > stop, position - stop, 0.0))\n```\n\niii. Same signed-distance computation as the reference: `position - zone_start` when before, `position - zone_end` when after, 0 when inside.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 7 bins using explicit boundary conditions:\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_reward_distance(distance: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.empty(distance.shape, dtype=np.int16)\n    out[distance < -50] = 0\n    out[(distance >= -50) & (distance <= -10)] = 1\n    out[(distance > -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    out[distance == 0] = 3\n    out[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    out[distance > 50] = 6\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The AI uses explicit boolean indexing rather than `np.digitize`. The boundary conditions differ slightly from the reference (e.g., -10 goes to bin 1 in the AI but bin 2 in the reference; 50 goes to bin 5 in the AI but bin 6 in the reference).\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as neural data within each trial, using the same `[start:stop]` slice.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = b[\"position\"][start:stop]\ndistance = reward_distance(position, zone)\n```\n\niii. Same frame-level alignment as all other variables.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = b[\"position\"][start:stop]\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable records the animal's position in the virtual corridor in cm.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing.\n\nii.\n```python\ndiscretize_position(position)\n```\n\niii. Raw position values are used directly.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins using explicit boundary conditions:\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.zeros(position.shape, dtype=np.int16)\n    out[(position >= 90) & (position < 180)] = 1\n    out[(position >= 180) & (position < 270)] = 2\n    out[(position >= 270) & (position <= 360)] = 3\n    out[position > 360] = 4\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Five 90-cm bins spanning the 450-cm track. The AI's boundary handling uses left-closed, right-open intervals for most bins, with `[270, 360]` closed on both sides. The reference uses `np.digitize` with slightly different boundary behavior.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as the neural data within each trial.\n\nii. Same `[start:stop]` indexing.\n\niii. Verified by shared frame indices.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick = b[\"lick\"][start:stop]\n```\n\niii. The `lick` variable records cumulative lick counts per imaging frame.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Binarized: any positive lick value is mapped to 1, otherwise 0.\n\nii.\n```python\n(lick > 0).astype(np.int16)\n```\n\niii. Matches the instructions for binary lick output (no/yes).\n\n## 9-c. Per-trial binary output broadcast across all timepoints.\n\nii.\n```python\noutcomes = np.array([\n    np.any((reward_times >= timestamps[start]) & (reward_times < timestamps[stop]))\n    for start, stop in zip(starts, stops)\n], dtype=np.int16)\n...\nnp.full(stop - start, outcomes[trial_idx], dtype=np.int16)\n```\n\niii. Three reward events outside all trial intervals are intentionally excluded. The reward rate is ~84.6%, matching the paper's ~15% omission rate.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: Data is cropped to the minimum common length across all neural and behavior arrays.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 2 samples are excluded.\n- **Non-scanning periods**: Trials where scanning is not 1 are excluded.\n- **Non-finite behavior**: Trials with NaN/Inf in required behavior variables are excluded.\n- **Corrupt lick sensor**: Trials where >30% of frames have lick count > 2 are excluded (81 trials total).\n- **Reward events outside trials**: Three inter-trial reward deliveries are not assigned to any trial.\n- **Subject ID verification**: Cross-checked between filename and NWB metadata.\n\nii.\n```python\ncommon_length = min([behavior_len] + [x.data.shape[0] for x in fluorescence_series] +\n                    [x.data.shape[0] for x in neuropil_series])\n...\nif stop - start < 2:\n    reason = \"fewer than two samples\"\nelif not np.all(b[\"scanning\"][start:stop] == 1):\n    reason = \"outside valid scanning period\"\n...\nlick_bad_fraction = float(np.mean(b[\"lick\"][start:stop] > 2))\nif lick_bad_fraction > 0.30:\n    reason = \"corrupt lick sensor (>30% frames with count >2)\"\n```\n\niii.
